From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF31.1, F30.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m here mostly because everybody around me seems to think I’m doing too much, or taking on too much, or whatever phrase they keep using. But from where I’m standing, it just feels like I’m the only one with enough push to get things done properly.\n\nWe’ve got this move to handle, and if I don’t stay on top of every box, every bit of planning, every little thing that has to be measured and arranged, it won’t get done right. And then there’s the new garage — I mean, if you’re going to rebuild a garage, you don’t just slap boards together and call it finished. It needs to be done right. I’ve been thinking through the layout, the tools, where everything should go, how it ought to work.\n\nAnd on top of that, there are things at church that need organizing, and people act like that’s extra, but somebody has to keep things moving. Same at work, too. I may be 74, but I still know how the job ought to be done, and these younger fellows, they need showing. They move too slow or they don’t see the details.\n\nSo, I guess that’s what brings me in — other people saying I’m running myself too hard. I don’t really see it that way. I feel like I’ve got the drive, and there’s a lot that needs doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way exactly. I mean, I’ve never been one to lie around half the morning anyway, you know? I get up when there’s work to do, and lately there’s been plenty to do. So if I’m awake, I usually just figure, well, might as well get moving, might as well think through the next step or go over what needs doing.\n\nBut whether I’m “needing less sleep” — I don’t know. I haven’t really sat there counting hours like that. My wife would probably have some opinion on it, but to me it just feels like there’s too much waiting on me to waste time fussing over sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more confident than usual in your abilities lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have, though I wouldn’t call it “too confident” the way other people might. It’s more that I can see what needs doing, and I know I’m the one who can get it lined up right. Lately I’ve been feeling like people either need to get on board with the plan or just move aside, because all this second-guessing and slow walking things just gums up the works.\n\nAnd I’ll admit, I’ve been feeling pretty big about it sometimes — joking around, feeling sharp, like I’m ten feet tall and nobody can really keep up with me. But then someone asks what I think is a silly question — my wife, or my boy — and I can snap at them before I even think much about it. It’s not that I mean to be harsh, exactly, but when I’m trying to get something done and people keep questioning every step, it gets under my skin fast.\n\nDoctor: Is this burst of energy and drive a clear change from how you usually are?\n\nPatient: Yes… I’d say it is different, if I’m being fair about it. I’ve always been someone who keeps busy — I’m not a sit-around kind of man — but lately it’s like I can’t stay planted in a chair for more than a few minutes. I’ll sit down thinking I’m going to rest, and then I’m back up again sorting through boxes, checking what goes where, measuring something for the garage, loading tools, moving things around, or deciding I need to go back over to the old place because I remembered one more thing that ought to be handled.\n\nIt’s not just “keeping busy,” exactly. It feels like my body has a motor in it. Like if I’m not doing something, something’s wrong. And there’s so much to get lined up that sitting still feels almost impossible. My wife notices it, I think, because I’ll say I’m going to sit for a minute and then I’m gone again, off doing the next thing before anyone even finishes talking. So yes, compared to my usual, this is… more. More drive, more motion, more being up and at it.\n\nDoctor: Has this burst of energy caused serious problems in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess if I’m being honest, it has. I don’t like saying that, because in my mind I was just trying to get things handled, but it got to the point where other people weren’t seeing it that way at all.\n\nAt work, there was a safety incident — and I don’t really want to dress it up like it was nothing, because they pulled me from work after it. That’s not a small thing. I’ve been around equipment and shop floors long enough to know safety matters, but at the time I felt like I knew what I was doing and could keep everything moving. Then afterward, suddenly I’m being told I can’t be there doing my job, and that hit me hard. It felt insulting at first, like people were overreacting, but… I can see now it was serious.\n\nAnd at home it’s been bad too. My wife and the family, they stopped trusting me with decisions — money, driving, things with the move, all of it. I’d say I had a plan, and they’d look at each other like they needed to step in before I made it worse. That’s a terrible feeling, having your own family act like you can’t be counted on to make normal decisions. But I suppose things had gotten out of hand enough that they felt they had to.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you had experiences that other people said were not real, such as hearing voices or having beliefs they found unrealistic?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d put it that way. I mean, nobody’s told me I was hearing voices or seeing things that weren’t there, not like that. I haven’t had some voice talking to me from the wall or anything of that sort.\n\nAs for beliefs being unrealistic… well, people have certainly acted like some of my ideas were too much, or like I was overestimating what I could get done. My family especially would say, “You can’t do all that,” or “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d think they were just being cautious or slowing me down. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nI felt very sure of myself, maybe more sure than they were comfortable with, but I wasn’t… I wasn’t having some experience where I thought the television was sending me messages or strangers were following me, if that’s what you’re asking. At least not that I can recall.\n\nDoctor: During this high-energy period, did you ever become convinced of something others said was impossible or clearly untrue?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that, no. I mean, I definitely got fixed on ideas — things I thought needed doing right then, plans I thought made perfect sense — and other people would push back pretty hard. My wife especially would look at me like, “What are you talking about?” or tell me I was taking on too much.\n\nBut convinced of something impossible? Like something that just flat-out couldn’t be true? I don’t think so. At least, I can’t point to something and say, “Yes, I believed that even though everyone said it was clearly untrue.” It was more that I was very sure I could handle things, or that my way was the right way, and people around me thought I was being unreasonable. Maybe there’s a fine line there, I don’t know, but I don’t remember believing anything bizarre or impossible like that.\n\nDoctor: At any other time in your life, have you gone through a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had low times, sure — at my age I think most people have had stretches where things feel heavy, or you’re worn down, or you don’t feel like yourself. But if you’re asking whether there was a clear two-week period where I was depressed most of the day nearly every day… I don’t know that I can point to one and say yes.\n\nI’m not saying I’ve never felt sad or discouraged. I have. But I don’t remember a stretch like that where it was that constant, day after day, all day long. At least not in a way that stands out to me as something I’d call a real episode. It’s more like there have been hard days, or maybe a few days here and there where I’m down, but two weeks straight… I don’t think so. Not that I can clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this recent high-energy spell, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt unusually energized and driven like this?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so — not quite as sharp in my memory as this recent spell, but there have been times where I got that same kind of… motor feeling. Like I couldn’t just sit down and stay put.\n\nI’d sit in the chair for maybe a few minutes, and then I’d be up again — sorting boxes, moving things around, measuring out space in the garage like something had to be planned right then, loading tools into the truck, then thinking I needed to go back over to the old place to check on something or get something else. It wasn’t just being busy in the normal way. It was like my body wouldn’t agree to rest. I’d tell myself, “Just sit down for a bit,” and then almost right away I’d be standing up and doing the next thing.\n\nSo, yes, I guess I have had periods like that before, where I was unusually driven and couldn’t leave things alone. It’s hard to say exactly how it compares, but that feeling of being pushed from one task to the next — I do recognize that.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you ever had a period when you lost interest or pleasure in almost everything?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you’re asking. I mean, I’ve had times where I didn’t feel like doing much, or where things felt kind of flat for a day or two, especially if I was tired or stressed, but “lost interest or pleasure in almost everything” sounds stronger than what I can really remember.\n\nI still usually had something I cared about or could get myself into, even if I wasn’t exactly cheerful. Maybe I’d be less enthusiastic, or I’d put things off, but I don’t recall a period where basically nothing mattered or nothing felt enjoyable across the board. If it happened, it’s not standing out to me as a clear spell. So I’d say no, or at least not that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I know you asked something very close to that a minute ago, and I’m trying not to overcomplicate it, but… I don’t think I can honestly say yes.\n\nI’ve definitely had days where I felt low or discouraged, or where I woke up and thought, “I don’t feel like dealing with anything today.” And at this age, you collect enough disappointments and stress that you’re not cheerful all the time. At least, I can’t point to something and say, “Yes, I believed that even though everyone said it was clearly untrue.” It was more that I was very sure I could handle things, or that my way was the right way, and people around me thought I was being unreasonable. Maybe there’s a fine line there, I don’t know, but I don’t remember believing anything bizarre or impossible like that.\n\nDoctor: At any other time in your life, have you gone through a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had low times, sure — at my age I think most people have had stretches where things feel heavy, or you’re worn down, or you don’t feel like yourself. But if you’re asking whether there was a clear two-week period where I was depressed most of the day nearly every day… I don’t know that I can point to one and say yes.\n\nI’m not saying I’ve never felt sad or discouraged. I have. But I don’t remember a stretch like that where it was that constant, day after day, all day long. At least not in a way that stands out to me as something I’d call a real episode. It’s more like there have been hard days, or maybe a few days here and there where I’m down, but two weeks straight… I don’t think so. Not that I can clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this recent high-energy spell, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt unusually energized and driven like this?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so — not quite as sharp in my memory as this recent spell, but there have been times where I got that same kind of… motor feeling. Like I couldn’t just sit down and stay put.\n\nI’d sit in the chair for maybe a few minutes, and then I’d be up again — sorting boxes, moving things around, measuring out space in the garage like something had to be planned right then, loading tools into the truck, then thinking I needed to go back over to the old place to check on something or get something else. It wasn’t just being busy in the normal way. It was like my body wouldn’t agree to rest. I’d tell myself, “Just sit down for a bit,” and then almost right away I’d be standing up and doing the next thing.\n\nSo, yes, I guess I have had periods like that before, where I was unusually driven and couldn’t leave things alone. It’s hard to say exactly how it compares, but that feeling of being pushed from one task to the next — I do recognize that.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you ever had a period when you lost interest or pleasure in almost everything?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you’re asking. Before this one? I’m not sure I can give you a clean answer on that.

I mean, I’ve had times in my life where I was busy, or worked hard, or got really focused on getting something done. With the kind of work I’ve done, there are stretches where you just push through because that’s what the job or the situation calls for. And I’ve always been the sort of person who, once I get my mind set on something, I can stay with it longer than maybe some people would.

But if you mean something like this recent spell — unusually energized, driven in a way that felt noticeably different, and lasting a whole week or more — I don’t really have another example that jumps out at me. Not one I can confidently say was the same kind of thing. I’m trying to think back, but nothing clear comes to mind. So I’d say I don’t know of another time before this, at least not that I can clearly remember.

Lately I’ve been feeling like people either need to get on board with the plan or just move aside, because all this second-guessing and slow walking things just gums up the works.\n\nAnd I’ll admit, I’ve been feeling pretty big about it sometimes — joking around, feeling sharp, like I’m ten feet tall and nobody can really keep up with me. But then someone asks what I think is a silly question — my wife, or my boy — and I can snap at them before I even think much about it. It’s not that I mean to be harsh, exactly, but when I’m trying to get something done and people keep questioning every step, it gets under my skin fast.\n\nDoctor: Is this burst of energy and drive a clear change from how you usually are?\n\nPatient: Yes… I’d say it is different, if I’m being fair about it. I’ll sit down thinking I’m going to rest, and then I’m back up again sorting through boxes, checking what goes where, measuring something for the garage, loading tools, moving things around, or deciding I need to go back over to the old place because I remembered one more thing that ought to be handled.\n\nIt’s not just “keeping busy,” exactly. It feels like my body has a motor in it. Like if I’m not doing something, something’s wrong. And there’s so much to get lined up that sitting still feels almost impossible. So yes, compared to my usual, this is… more. I don’t like saying that, because in my mind I was just trying to get things handled, but it got to the point where other people weren’t seeing it that way at all.\n\nAt work, there was a safety incident — and I don’t really want to dress it up like it was nothing, because they pulled me from work after it. That’s not a small thing. I’ve been around equipment and shop floors long enough to know safety matters, but at the time I felt like I knew what I was doing and could keep everything moving. Then afterward, suddenly I’m being told I can’t be there doing my job, and that hit me hard. It felt insulting at first, like people were overreacting, but… I can see now it was serious.\n\nAnd at home it’s been bad too. My wife and the family, they stopped trusting me with decisions — money, driving, things with the move, all of it. I’d say I had a plan, and they’d look at each other like they needed to step in before I made it worse. That’s a terrible feeling, having your own family act like you can’t be counted on to make normal decisions. But I suppose things had gotten out of hand enough that they felt they had to.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you had experiences that other people said were not real, such as hearing voices or having beliefs they found unrealistic?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d put it that way. I mean, nobody’s told me I was hearing voices or seeing things that weren’t there, not like that. I haven’t had some voice talking to me from the wall or anything of that sort.\n\nAs for beliefs being unrealistic… well, people have certainly acted like some of my ideas were too much, or like I was overestimating what I could get done. My family especially would say, “You can’t do all that,” or “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d think they were just being cautious or slowing me down. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nI felt very sure of myself, maybe more sure than they were comfortable with, but I wasn’t… I wasn’t having some experience where I thought the television was sending me messages or strangers were following me, if that’s what you’re asking. At least not that I can recall.\n\nDoctor: During this high-energy period, did you ever become convinced of something others said was impossible or clearly untrue?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that, no. I mean, I definitely got fixed on ideas — things I thought needed doing right then, plans I thought made perfect sense — and other people would push back pretty hard. My wife especially would look at me like, “What are you talking about?” or tell me I was taking on too much.\n\nBut convinced of something impossible? Like something that just flat-out couldn’t be true? I don’t think so. At least, I can’t point to something and say, “Yes, I believed that even though everyone said it was clearly untrue.” It was more that I was very sure I could handle things, or that my way was the right way, and people around me thought I was being unreasonable. Maybe there’s a fine line there, I don’t know, but I don’t remember believing anything bizarre or impossible like that.\n\nDoctor: At any other time in your life, have you gone through a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had low times, sure — at my age I think most people have had stretches where things feel heavy, or you’re worn down, or you don’t feel like yourself. But if you’re asking whether there was a clear two-week period where I was depressed most of the day nearly every day… I don’t know that I can point to one and say yes.\n\nI’m not saying I’ve never felt sad or discouraged. I have. But I don’t remember a stretch like that where it was that constant, day after day, all day long. At least not in a way that stands out to me as something I’d call a real episode. It’s more like there have been hard days, or maybe a few days here and there where I’m down, but two weeks straight… I don’t think so. Not that I can clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this recent high-energy spell, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt unusually energized and driven like this?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so — not quite as sharp in my memory as this recent spell, but there have been times where I got that same kind of… motor feeling. Like I couldn’t just sit down and stay put.\n\nI’d sit in the chair for maybe a few minutes, and then I’d be up again — sorting boxes, moving things around, measuring out space in the garage like something had to be planned right then, loading tools into the truck, then thinking I needed to go back over to the old place to check on something or get something else. It wasn’t just being busy in the normal way. It was like my body wouldn’t agree to rest. I’d tell myself, “Just sit down for a bit,” and then almost right away I’d be standing up and doing the next thing.\n\nSo, yes, I guess I have had periods like that before, where I was unusually driven and couldn’t leave things alone. It’s hard to say exactly how it compares, but that feeling of being pushed from one task to the next — I do recognize that.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you ever had a period when you lost interest or pleasure in almost everything?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you’re asking. Maybe I’d be less enthusiastic, or I’d put things off, but I don’t recall a period where basically nothing mattered or nothing felt enjoyable across the board. If it happened, it’s not standing out to me as a clear spell. So I’d say no, or at least not that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I know you asked something very close to that a minute ago, and I’m trying not to overcomplicate it, but… I don’t think I can honestly say yes.\n\nI’ve definitely had days where I felt low or discouraged, or where I woke up and thought, “I don’t feel like dealing with anything today.” And at this age, you collect enough disappointments and stress that you’re not cheerful all the time. It wasn’t just a couple of days where I had extra energy and then settled down. I don’t think I had that kind of sustained depressed period, not nearly every day for two weeks.
